## Learnings and Observations

- 2026-07-16 LA-Minnesota settlement: Minnesota won 96-87; Q1 was 30, halftime 76 and full total 183. The selected card went 1-3: 1H Over 88.5 LOST, Full Under 181.5 LOST by 1.5, Q1 Over 44.0 LOST, and forced Full Over 181.5 WON; Minnesota winner WON. The phase model over-weighted LA's L5/L10 fast-start environment and did not give Minnesota's opening defensive suppression enough weight without Collier. The back-to-back fatigue hypothesis appeared late rather than early: the first half produced only 76, then the game added 107 after halftime. New WNBA pregame correction: when a strong home favorite has a credible defensive identity but key availability creates uncertainty, do not stack Q1 and 1H Overs from the underdog's recent phase form alone; require matchup-level pace/shot-quality support, and keep the correlated phase selections below 55% if the direct H2H only cleared the half line by 0.5. Separately, a near-line full Under cannot be treated as robust when its own complement is forced into the card and both teams retain a 100+ second-half tail through adjustment, transition and late fouling.
- 2026-07-16 France-Spain settlement: Spain won 2-0; Spain team Under 2.5, match Under 4.5 and corners Under 13.5 all won, while Mbappe 1+ SOT lost and France to advance lost. The broad ceiling structure was strong and the low-corner path repeated through opponent suppression: eight total corners despite France taking seven. The player-prop confidence was too high because Mbappe's exceptional recent SOT hit rate was not discounted enough against Spain's tournament allowance of only 1.17 opponent SOT per match; he took three shots but put none on target. New soccer calibration: when an elite defense has a demonstrated opponent-SOT suppression mechanism, do not price a star's 1+ SOT above the high-60s from hit rate alone; model shot quality, transition access and likely defensive game state explicitly. Exact-winner caution was justified, but France was still the wrong advancement side.
- 2026-07-15 France-Spain pre-card retrospective gate: every earlier active card was final, so none was skipped as live. The closest World Cup lessons were Norway-England and Argentina-Switzerland, both 1-1 in regulation with 4-0 safety cards and the favourites advancing only after 90 minutes, plus Spain-Belgium, where Spain 4+ team corners won but match corners Over 8.0 lost because Belgium managed one corner. Current application: keep regulation ceilings, a high-volume named-player SOT floor and a broad corner ceiling above an exact regulation winner; the separate potential winner is France to advance, not France to win in 90 minutes. No new universal rule is added because this directly reinforces the existing regulation/advance split and team-corner-versus-opponent-suppression rule.
- 2026-07-13 pending settlement sweep: all three active cards are now closed. Houston-Texas finished 3-1 and Milwaukee-Pittsburgh plus Seattle-Washington each finished 2-2 across their selected four, for seven wins and five losses overall. All three potential winners won. The two material calibration misses were (a) burying Milwaukee-Pittsburgh Over 8.5 despite a replacement starter, hot Pittsburgh scoring and a doubleheader-taxed staff, and (b) leaving Seattle-Washington 1H Under 83.5 above its Over after a 14-point opening 2:34 and Seattle's 4/5 recent first-half Over record.
- 2026-07-13 MLB Milwaukee-Pittsburgh settlement: Pittsburgh won 14-5 after four runs in the second and ten in the fourth. Pirates +1.5 and the Pittsburgh winner won; Brewers +1.5 and Under 8.5 lost; bottom Over 8.5 won. Robert Gasser allowed seven earned runs in three innings, then Jared Koenig allowed five earned runs without recording an out. The model named starter/bullpen failure as the Over route but still ranked Over last, repeating the MLB bottom-slot leak. New MLB rule: after a doubleheader, a late replacement starter plus a thinned bridge can create early-middle-inning bullpen exposure, not merely late-inning risk; do not top-rank an under from the replacement's recent ERA when opponent L10/L15 totals and staff availability support multiple 9+ paths.
- 2026-07-13 MLB Houston-Texas settlement: Texas won 6-5 on Brandon Nimmo's walk-off single. Astros +1.5, Over 9.0 and Rangers ML all won; Under lost. Javier was correctly treated as a short starter (3 IP, 3 ER), Gore lasted four innings, and the bullpens covered the remaining eleven innings between them. Houston's cushion and the Texas winner coexisted in the one-run corridor, while Josh Hader allowed three straight ninth-inning hits without recording an out. This validates ranking broad cushion/whole-number Over structure above a narrow winner while still keeping the winner live.
- 2026-07-13 WNBA Seattle-Washington settlement: Washington won 84-79; phase totals were Q1 42, halftime 85 and full 163. Q1 Over 41 and 1H Over 83.5 won; Full Under 162.5 lost by 0.5 and 1H Under 83.5 lost by 1.5. The Q1 live arithmetic was right but fragile by one point. The ranking error was failing to propagate the elevated Q1 projection into the half: once 14 points arrived in 2:34 and Q1 centred 44-47, the 1H Over needed only an ordinary second quarter, while Seattle's recent first halves were already Over 83.5 in four of five. New WNBA live-phase rule: after a fast opening changes the Q1 centre, recompute the exact Q2 requirement and promote 1H Over above Under unless live possession/shot-quality evidence shows a real slowdown; a separate full Under can remain viable because Q3/Q4 and rotation effects differ.
- 2026-07-13 Brisbane-Essendon settlement: Brisbane won 149-59, total 208. The halftime card went 1-1 with top-pick loss and bottom-pick win: Under 180.5 LOST and Over 160.5 WON. From 76 at halftime, the game added 132 second-half points; quarter totals were 39, 37, 49 and 83. Brisbane sustained the named territory/blowout route (69-39 inside 50s, 20-11 marks inside 50 and 44-30 clearances), while Essendon simultaneously produced the named rebound route with 44 fourth-quarter points after only 15 through three quarters. The model correctly identified both failure mechanisms but still left Under 180.5 at 76-80%, a probability/rank calibration error. AFL live correction: when a favourite leads by 50+ at halftime with extreme inside-50 dominance, widen the second-half scoring distribution materially and do not top-rank an under merely because it has a larger arithmetic cushion; explicitly price the favourite continuing for percentage and the trailing side's low-base scoring rebound, which can occur together.
- 2026-07-12 pending-queue settlement: the six completed cards produced 19 wins and five losses across 24 ranked picks (79.2%), with four 4-0 cards, one 2-2 card and one 1-3 card. Potential winners went 5-1. Brisbane-Essendon was rechecked at 18:15 AEST and remained live in the third quarter, so it was not graded or retrospectively analysed.
- 2026-07-12 NBA Summer League cleanup: Philadelphia beat Indiana 100-93 in target-score overtime after 93-93 regulation; Q1 was 58, halftime 104 and regulation total 186, so all four selections won without needing overtime for the total. San Antonio beat New York 70-49 with phase totals 26/56/119, sweeping the under/winner card through genuinely poor efficiency: New York shot 26.5% and committed 22 turnovers. Denver beat Minnesota 101-82 with phase totals 39/91/183; only the full Under 185.5 won. Minnesota ML and -5.5 were assigned far too much confidence from one current-roster game. Summer League rule: a single opener cannot support a high-60s side probability without confirmed rotation continuity and a durable role/quality mismatch; cap the side, preserve phase/full-total independence, and price shooting/bench regression explicitly.
- 2026-07-12 World Cup quarter-final cleanup: Norway-England and Argentina-Switzerland both ended 1-1 in regulation before the favourites advanced in extra time. Both four-pick safety cards swept and both advance leans won. Haaland recorded two regulation shots on target; Norway-England cleared 7.5 regulation corners narrowly, while Argentina-Switzerland stayed below 13.5 even including extra time. This reinforces the existing rule: separate regulation winner from advance, and keep player SOT/team-goal/double-chance/goal-ceiling structures above an exact regulation favourite when a resilient underdog can force extra time.
- 2026-07-12 MLC settlement correction: Texas finished 165 all out after a 66/0 powerplay and 93/1 start; Washington chased 166/7 in 19.4. The original full Under 179.5 and PP Over 54.5 won, their complements lost, and Washington won. The interim live Over lean at 93/1 was wrong because Texas lost all ten wickets and only added 72 runs after that state, including five wickets for 33 in the final five overs. Live T20 projection must re-anchor immediately after clustered wickets using set-batter quality and remaining specialist depth; a hot wicketless powerplay is evidence for that phase, not permission to carry the run rate through 20 overs.
- 2026-07-12 MLC live audit, Texas-Washington: the actual pre-match pitch report could not be verified. Willow's public pages did not expose the match-specific host-feed walkaround, 7plus was geo-blocked, and the checked official/team, Cricbuzz and CREX public pages did not supply an attributed same-day report. Therefore the earlier pregame card fails the new mandatory report gate and must not be treated as a fully validated cricket card. This is an access failure, not evidence about the pitch. Live evidence is separate: Washington chose to bowl; Texas reached 66/0 in the powerplay and 93/1 after 9.3 overs, with Faf du Plessis 43 off 28, Saiteja Mukkamalla out for 47 off 29, eight opening-stand sixes and a 9.79 run rate. PP Over 54.5 is settled WON and PP Under LOST. At 93/1, Over 179.5 needed 87 from 63 legal balls (8.29 per over) and the live projection rose to about 195, but Texas then collapsed to 165 all out; that live Over lean was wrong and is corrected in the settlement note above. For every future cricket game: no verified match-specific same-day actual report means no final four-pick pregame card—only a provisional watchlist. Verify teams, competition, venue, date/start window, source/reporter, timestamp and strip identity; never relabel generic preview copy or live scoring as the pitch report.
- 2026-07-12 Brewers-Pirates Game 1 settlement: Pittsburgh won 7-6, so both +1.5 cushions and Over 8.5 won while Under lost; the ranked card finished 3-1 and the narrow Milwaukee winner lean failed. Starter suppression did not hold: Ashcraft allowed five runs in five innings and Sproat lasted only three innings; Milwaukee reliever Aaron Ashby then allowed three runs in Pittsburgh's four-run seventh. MLB lesson: the overlapping +1.5 structure was correctly stronger than the exact winner, and the over correctly captured starter/bullpen failure paths. When the favourite's winner edge is small and both +1.5 sides rank above it, describe the winner as no more than a lean and make the late-inning bridge—not generic full-staff ERA—the decisive tiebreaker.
- 2026-07-12 Adelaide-Gold Coast settlement: Adelaide won 128-49, total 177 and margin 79. Over 156.5 and Adelaide -20.5 won; Gold Coast +20.5 and Under lost; Adelaide winner won. The total model and winner were correct, but the final live recheck wrongly moved Gold Coast +20.5 above Adelaide -20.5 after only one early Suns goal even while saying six points were insufficient to replace the pregame model. AFL live lesson: do not flip a near-coinflip handicap ranking from a single opening goal when the favourite still has the stronger four-quarter scoring, availability and opponent-collapse mechanisms; require sustained territory/inside-50 evidence or a materially larger scoreboard move.
- 2026-07-11 Southland-Nelson settlement: Southland won 83-71; Q1 was 46, halftime 95 and the final total 154. The live card went 3-1: Southland winner, Q1 Over 45 and full Under 183.5 won; 1H Under 91.5 lost. The in-turn Q1 reversal correctly responded to exact live arithmetic, and the broad full under captured Nelson's second-half collapse, but the halftime under underweighted Nelson's 27-point Q2 after a 24-point Q1. NZNBL phase lesson: once the opening quarter is already above the pregame phase mean, do not keep a 1H under materially above 55% unless the live possession/shot-quality feed shows a real slowdown mechanism.
- 2026-07-11 GWS-Geelong settlement: GWS won 86-73, total 159. Under 190.5 and GWS +5.5 won; Geelong -5.5 and Over 190.5 lost; the Geelong winner lean failed. Geelong led 49-24 at halftime but was outscored 62-24 after halftime. The under was robust, but the side call again over-weighted broad model consensus and underweighted GWS's 6-2 home record plus four straight H2H wins. AFL lesson: when model consensus conflicts with a strong current home split and repeated matchup success, cap the away favourite below 55% and keep the home cushion above the favourite cover unless availability provides a concrete four-quarter territory advantage.
- 2026-07-11 Chicago-Los Angeles settlement: the Sparks won 102-87; Q1 was 57, halftime 101 and the final total 189. LA moneyline and full Over 176.5 won, while both phase unders and the live Q1 Under 45.5 lost. WNBA correction: do not promote a pooled L10/L20 phase-under percentage when one team's L5 phase environment is sharply over and the live opening immediately consumes the improved-line cushion. Treat a 45.5 live Q1 under as a pass once a neutral remaining-rate projection centres on 46; missing creators do not automatically imply low efficiency when healthy veterans redistribute usage successfully.
- 2026-07-11 Spain-Belgium settlement: Spain won 2-1 with a 5-1 corner split and Oyarzabal one shot on target. The main card swept 4-0 and Spain advanced, while the separate match-corners Over 8.0 lost at six. Soccer repeat lesson: when one favourite has a proven team-corner floor but also suppresses opponent corners, prefer the favourite team-corner line to a match-corner over that requires underdog contribution; whole-number push protection does not create an edge if the opponent cannot generate territory.
- 2026-07-11 Collingwood-North Melbourne settlement: Collingwood won 89-85, total 174. The pregame Under 173.5 and Collingwood -16.5 lost, North +16.5 and the bottom Over 173.5 won, while the Collingwood winner lean was correct. Both live Under 181.5 calls won. AFL correction: when the favourite is missing key defenders and the underdog restores multiple forwards at a familiar indoor venue, do not let recent underdog scoring collapse make a moderate pregame under the anchor without a stronger territory-suppression mechanism; distinguish the near-line pregame under from a later live under supported by actual second-quarter slowdown and exact remaining-points arithmetic.
- 2026-07-10 England-India 4th T20I cleanup: India finished 158/7 after a 44/2 powerplay and England chased 159/1 in 13.5 overs. The two top-ranked India unders won, both over complements lost, and the England winner lean was correct. This reinforces the existing T20 phase/full-innings separation: warm, dry conditions do not create a high total by themselves when the opponent has a repeatable short-ball/slower-ball plan, early wickets land, and only one batter supplies sustained acceleration.
- 2026-07-08 KBO cleanup: Samsung beat LG 9-2, and KT beat Kiwoom 3-0. Samsung/over validated the hot-home-lineup plus park/bullpen-exposure path, while KT/under validated starter suppression plus weak Kiwoom scoring. KBO lesson split: direct starter-vs-opponent history is not enough to protect a road +1.5 when current form, park, hot bats and bullpen exposure all point to the home side; separately, a strong underdog starter can support the under without making that underdog +1.5 equally strong if the lineup cannot convert traffic into runs.
- 2026-07-08 later KBO cleanup: KT beat Kiwoom 7-3, landing exactly 10 runs. KT ML and winner lean were right, Heroes +2.5 failed, and both 10.0 total sides pushed. Repeat KBO lesson: a wide +2.5 road cushion still fails when the favourite lineup creates repeated traffic and the underdog staff/defence allow multi-inning pressure; whole-number totals need explicit push-band treatment because early 6-run starts can still settle exactly on 10 after bullpens stabilize.
- 2026-07-08 State of Origin cleanup: NSW beat Queensland 30-12, so the Queensland cover/top pick and under both failed, while NSW +5.5 and Over 44.5 won. Repeat rugby-league lesson: do not let home-decider history outrank live selection/tactical upside when the underdog has elite halves and a credible pressure-kicking/control path; Game 2 blowout form can overstate favourite continuity if the opponent's spine changes solve the ruck/field-position problem.
- 2026-07-08 Wimbledon tennis cleanup: Zverev beat Fritz 6-4, 6-4, 6-2. Zverev +1.5 and Under 44.5 won, while the top-ranked Over 44.5 and Fritz -1.5/winner lean lost. Repeat tennis lesson: H2H and serve-heavy grass history can point to long-match structure, but current form/physical read can flip the match into a short straight-sets result; do not rank a high games over #1 when one player's clean-return/physical-separation path is visible and the line requires four tight sets or five sets.
- 2026-07-09 pending sweep soccer cleanup: Argentina beat Egypt 3-2 and Switzerland advanced over Colombia on penalties after 0-0. Argentina-Egypt reinforced that a knockout favourite can win/score while both high-ish corner and Under 3.5 legs fail: corner overs need two-sided corner contribution, not just territorial dominance, and Under 3.5 can be fragile when the underdog has live comeback/transition goals. Switzerland-Colombia reinforced the opposite low-event knockout path: low total and draw-protected regulation structures can be right while advance/winner leans remain fragile in penalty branches.
- 2026-07-10 AFL pending cleanup: Fremantle beat Sydney 15.21 (111) to 10.13 (73) after trailing 0.11 to 4.9 at halftime. Sydney +14.5 failed, the Over 173.5 and Fremantle -14.5 won, and the Fremantle winner lean was right. AFL lesson: a top-side underdog cushion can be correct in principle but still fail if the favourite has a dominant inside-50/clearance second-half response and the underdog's early pressure cannot be sustained; when the favourite's home scoring history includes repeated 30+ and 50+ wins, do not rank the underdog cushion above the favourite cover unless the underdog has a reliable four-quarter territory and scoring-resistance mechanism.
- 2026-07-08 Switzerland-Colombia settlement: the card went 4-0 on listed markets but missed the advance/winner lean. Switzerland-Colombia finished 0-0 through regulation and extra time, Switzerland advanced 4-3 on penalties, and ESPN's boxscore listed 10 regulation corners. Repeat soccer lesson: low-total/non-loss/corner structure can be correct while the exact advance lean fails; keep advance/winner confidence capped in even knockout games unless the chance-quality edge is much clearer.
- 2026-07-08 England-India T20I settlement: England made 201/7 after a 49-run powerplay, then bowled India out for 76. The PP under read was correct, but the full-innings under failed by 1.5 because Salt's platform and Curran/death hitting pushed the innings over 199.5. Cricket repeat lesson: at elite men's T20 venues, a PP under can win while a 200+ full-innings over also wins if wickets in hand and death hitters convert overs 16-20; do not rank full-innings under #1 from venue averages/chase bias alone when the written stress test already names a 200+ platform path.
- 2026-07-07 FIBA qualifier blowout-total lesson: New Zealand 129, Guam 75 — every "under" total/phase lean lost and every "over" (including both bottom-ranked legs) won. Do not assume blowout minutes suppress the total against a bottom-of-table/eliminated opponent with a large season point-differential gap (Guam were 0-5, PD -110); a favourite's bench can keep scoring efficiently in transition/garbage time when the trailing team has no size or depth at any rotation spot, so total/phase markets should lean OVER even deep into a probable rout. See row 2026-07-06 New Zealand vs Guam for full retro.
- Carry forward the v2 priority audit: the #4 / least-likely slot is still the weakest calibration area. Forced complement bottoms, especially exact Over/Under or handicap pairs, must be labelled honestly and should not be described as impossible.
- Latest carryover learning from v2: WNBA first-half overs can be the cleanest expression even when the full-game over is only a lean. Las Vegas-Phoenix cleared 1H Over 83.5 easily at 94, then slowed to a 162 final total.
- Current AFL carryover observation: a likely winner is not automatically the best handicap pick. Fremantle beat Geelong 99-90, validating Fremantle as winner but Geelong +19.5 as the stronger listed pick; the over also cleared because both sides scored enough, not because the favourite ran away.
- 2026-06-19 settlement sweep: in expanded-group soccer, draw-protected and goal-ceiling picks can be right even when the exact winner lean misses. Czechia-South Africa finished 1-1; the double chance and low-total/team-total structure was stronger than the narrow Czechia winner call.
- 2026-06-19 MLB update: protect the broad cushion and the cleaner total mechanism above exact winner calls when the favourite's offense is cold. Toronto +1.5/Under 9.5 and Minnesota +1.5/Over 7.5 both won from the top half; wind/roof conditions were modifiers, not primary anchors.
- 2026-06-19 cricket correction: for WT20I first-innings totals at 165+, good-pitch comments and batting depth are not enough without a clear powerplay plus wickets-in-hand path. West Indies made only 33 in the powerplay and 153/6, so the under won from the bottom slot.
- 2026-06-20 AFL repeat lesson: when the handicap is only a low single digit, a cleaner winner edge and a cleaner cover edge can align. Hawthorn beat Gold Coast 113-97, so the pregame Hawthorn -3.5 / Over 175.5 top half was right; the Suns home-response risk was real but did not outweigh Hawthorn's midfield/territory and structural profile.
- 2026-06-20 WT20I repeat lesson: when a women's T20 innings line is 170+ at Southampton/Rose Bowl and the batting side is missing a major ceiling player, the full-innings under can be the anchor even if the opponent is weaker. New Zealand made only 140/6 after a 31-run powerplay without Sophie Devine; top-order wickets and venue pace mattered more than reputation gap.
- 2026-06-20 soccer repeat lesson: team-total ceilings and non-loss picks remain the safest way to express a technical-edge side in group play. Morocco beat Scotland 1-0; Scotland TT Under 2.5 and Morocco/draw won without needing a high-goal script.
- 2026-06-20 KBO/NPB settlement update: distinguish the safe +1.5 cushion from the starter-edge winner path. LG +1.5/Under 8.5 won in a 3-2 KBO result, but Seibu's starter edge crushed Orix +1.5 in a 4-0 NPB result; home/H2H cushion is not enough if the cushioned side has a credible zero-to-one-run offensive path.
- 2026-06-20 soccer phase/total split: Turkiye-Paraguay validated 1H Over 0.5 on a 2nd-minute goal, but full Over 2.5 lost in a 1-0. Do not automatically convert early-goal probability into full-over confidence when the leading side can defend and the chasing side's pressure is low-quality.
- 2026-06-20 NZNBL correction: Auckland beat Hawke's Bay 85-74, so the winner lean and unders were right but -15.5 was too high. When the favourite is clear but the line exceeds season margin and a current-season close H2H, put total/phase unders above the large cover unless there is a concrete blowout catalyst.
- 2026-06-20 WT20I elite-vs-associate ceiling correction: Australia made 219/6 and 61 in the powerplay against Netherlands, so the top-ranked Under 197.5 failed while PP Over 56.5 and the bottom full-innings Over 197.5 both won. When India/Australia-level batting has already shown a 200+ template against the same associate attack, venue/weather under anchors need same-day swing control, early wickets, or a weakened top order before ranking above the elite ceiling.
- 2026-06-21 WT20I low-threshold Bangladesh batting correction: Bangladesh made 123/6 with a 23-run powerplay after falling to 13/3, so the 133.5 full under and 38.5 PP under both won. At 130s thresholds, prior collapse evidence and a Pakistan-style spin/seam wicket mix can make the under the anchor even if Shorna/Nigar create a late recovery.
- 2026-06-21 settlement sweep: Carlton beat GWS 88-65, validating the underdog cushion plus under and exposing the GWS winner lean. When the top handicap/total logic already says "competitive underdog and lower total," cap the exact favourite-winner confidence unless the favourite's scoring edge is much cleaner.
- 2026-06-21 NZNBL correction: Otago beat Taranaki 91-87, so Airs +4.5 won but both under leans failed. In NZNBL close-spread spots, the cushion can be the cleanest edge while first-half/full unders remain fragile if recent phase totals sit close to the line.
- 2026-06-21 soccer attack-profile update: Netherlands beat Sweden 5-1 after leading 2-0 at halftime, so 1H Over 0.5 and full Over 2.5 both won. When confirmed attacking lineups and defensive fragility are present on both sides, do not over-apply the "1H over does not imply full over" caution.
- 2026-06-21 WNBA repeat correction: Atlanta beat Indiana 113-96 with a 115-point first half. Dream +5.5, full Over 175.5, and 1H Over 89.5 all won; Indiana's early scoring did not protect the cover once turnovers and Atlanta transition/creator depth took over.
- 2026-06-21 WT20I England split lesson: England made exactly 51 in the powerplay but still reached 200/5 through middle/death hitting. A high powerplay under and high full-innings over can both be right when early wicket/availability suppresses PP pace but elite depth remains intact.
- 2026-06-21 MLB Wrigley correction: Toronto beat the Cubs 8-6, so the road +1.5 cushion and Over 9.5 won while the narrow Cubs winner lean failed. Crosswind is not automatic suppression when both starters allow traffic and late bullpen scoring/HR paths are live.
- 2026-06-21 AFL St Kilda-Bulldogs correction: the high total under was correctly top-ranked, but the side read was wrong. When the handicap is a low single digit but the opponent owns a stronger top-end midfield/clearance group and a recent H2H dominance profile, do not let better percentage/defensive season averages push the favourite cover above the underdog cushion without a clean scoring-conversion edge.
- 2026-06-21 WT20I Sri Lanka batting correction: Sri Lanka made 98 all out with a 23-run powerplay at Bristol. A batting-friendly label and dry weather do not protect a 150+ innings line when the opponent chooses to bowl, opens with a world-class spin/all-round option, adds extra spin, and the top order loses wickets immediately.
- 2026-06-22 WT20I India-South Africa correction: India cleared the powerplay over with 59 but stalled to 158/7 and South Africa chased 161/4. Do not convert an elite powerplay automatically into a 165+ full-innings over when the opponent has enough attack depth to take wickets from overs 6-15 and the batting side lacks a late 45+ death surge.
- 2026-06-22 MLB roof/lineup correction: Houston beat Cleveland 2-1, so Guardians +1.5 and Astros ML both won while the Over 8.5 lost from the #2 slot and the bottom Under 8.5 won. A roof-closed, wind-neutral park plus two starters capable of stabilising can overpower recent bad starter logs, especially when one lineup has a thin lower third. Do not rank a modest MLB over above a narrow winner path unless the confirmed lineups and bullpen state both support 9+ runs.
- 2026-06-22 settlement sweep: Bangladesh-Australia 3rd T20I, KIA-KT, LAKR-Seattle, Washington-Minnesota and Mets-Phillies are now settled. Main corrections: live cricket starts must be reranked immediately after confirmed early wickets; WNBA favourite 1H overs and big covers need a hard missing-star/frontcourt stress test; KBO over + underdog cushion can outrank home favourite ML in hot H2H/offense environments; whole-number MLB totals deserve push-band treatment when ace run prevention and warm wind point to exactly 8.
- 2026-06-22 NZNBL correction: Southland beat Hawke's Bay 117-93 after a 102-point first half, so the winner lean was right but both under anchors and Hawks +12.5 failed. Do not top-rank recent team phase unders when an elite opponent has enough pace/scoring and H2H evidence to drag the game above the threshold and clear the large cover.
- 2026-06-22 NPB correction: Rakuten beat Seibu 8-7 in 12 innings after the teams were tied 6-6 through nine. Even with two elite starters, a very low 5.5 total is fragile when bullpen exposure and the full NPB extra-inning tail are live. Separate starter-run suppression from full-game total risk; at 5.5, the under needs both starter control and credible late-relief support.
- 2026-06-23 Israel basketball settlement: Galil beat Netanya 89-84 after trailing 46-44 at halftime. The full Under 173.5 and Galil -4.5 both won by only half a point, while the 1H over won. Treat exact-threshold wins as correct but fragile; the dead-rubber/rotation pace showed up early even though the final slowed.
- 2026-06-23 World Cup settlement: Argentina beat Austria 2-0 with Messi goals at 38' and 90+4'. The early-goal and full-under combination was correctly separated: a first-half goal does not require a three-goal full match when the favourite can control territory and protect a clean sheet.
- 2026-06-23 MLB Coors correction: Colorado beat Boston 3-2 on a three-run ninth-inning walk-off after Jake Bennett threw six scoreless innings with nine strikeouts. A tiny MLB sample of low strikeouts should not hard-cap a young starter's ceiling; at the same time, an underdog +1.5 cushion can survive and win even when a late bullpen collapse flips the exact winner.
- 2026-06-23 WNBA correction: Dallas beat Seattle 112-110 in overtime after 96-96 regulation. The 1H under won at 82, but the full under failed by 21.5 points before overtime. When both teams lose major defenders and usage concentrates in healthy scorers, do not let a prior low H2H or a weak L10 team average bury the full over; role-adjusted L5 player acceleration and replacement matchup are required.
- 2026-06-23 WT20I Bristol correction: Scotland reached 45/0 in the powerplay but stalled to 131/7 against New Zealand, so the powerplay over and full-innings under won together. A wicketless, boundary-rich first six does not guarantee a 137+ total when the batting side lacks deep acceleration and the fielding side can win overs 7-20 through spin, pace changes and boundary protection. This reinforces the existing cricket phase/full-innings split rule rather than creating a duplicate sports-doc rule.
- 2026-06-24 WT20I settlement sweep: Ireland made 30/3 and 130/5, validating the Ireland PP Under 49.5 plus innings Under 133.5 structure; three wickets inside the powerplay and Sri Lanka's stump-to-stump/spin control mattered more than Bristol's short-boundary scoring tail.
- 2026-06-24 soccer thin-sample correction: Buenos Aires City-Control Orientado finished 1-2 after a 0-1 halftime, so the amateur low-total profile failed. In thin lower-league cards, do not bury the away 2-1 pattern or unconfirmed-lineup volatility behind a small-sample home under trend.
- 2026-06-24 World Cup correction: Portugal beat Uzbekistan 5-0 after leading 3-0 at halftime. The 1H goal read was right, but the full Under 2.5 was too conservative for a favourite with confirmed attacking quality against a debutant defence once Portugal's opener chance-conversion concerns had a plausible finishing-rebound mechanism.
- 2026-06-24 WT20I elite-ceiling correction: Australia made 199/7 and 64 in the powerplay against Pakistan, so both Australia unders failed and the bottom full-over won. Against Australia-level batting, a 190s line can still clear when toss intent, venue scoring, prior 200+ ceiling, extras/fielding leakage and Perry/Gardner/Sutherland/Carey depth all point upward; a first-ball wicket alone does not kill the powerplay or innings over.
- 2026-06-24 U19 women's cricket correction: India U19 Women made only 134/6 after a 38-run powerplay against Sri Lanka U19 Women despite scoring 170/6 in the opener. In U19 women, direct same-series ceiling evidence must be balanced against XI rotation, missing prior-game scorers, venue average/pitch notes and slower strike-rate anchors; do not make a full-innings over the top pick unless the same batting core and powerplay intent are confirmed.
- 2026-06-25 pending cleanup: Southland beat Whai 102-96 in overtime after 81-81 regulation, turning the NZNBL full-game over from the bottom slot into a winner. FIBA-style domestic full totals that include OT need an explicit overtime-tail stress test when spread/quality context supports a close game, even if regulation scoring leans under.
- 2026-06-25 WT20I England-WI correction: England made 186/7 after only 46/2 in the powerplay. The powerplay under and full-innings over split was the right shape, but the full-innings under top slot was wrong because hot/dry conditions, short boundary pockets, Danni Wyatt-Hodge's anchor innings and West Indies fielding errors created a Lord's record ceiling despite Sciver-Brunt's absence.
- 2026-06-25 domestic basketball finals correction: Valencia beat Barcelona 108-84 with Q1 49, 1H 82 and full 192. Same-series slow Q1 evidence is not enough for a top-ranked Q1 under when an elimination/title game has a clear shot-making, turnover and defensive-collapse path; keep Q1 complements closer if the favourite/offence can dictate early.
- 2026-06-25 World Cup group split: Switzerland-Canada finished 2-1 after a 0-0 halftime, while Bosnia-Qatar finished 3-1 after a 2-1 halftime. In final group matches, separate first-half goal probability from full-game over: qualification/top-group risk management can create a scoreless first half even when second-half adjustments and chasing state push the full match over.
- 2026-06-25 full settlement sweep (Cleveland-CWS, Yankees-Tigers, Scotland-Brazil corners, Lynx-Mystics, Portland-Chicago, Braves-Padres, South Africa-South Korea): record across the 7 settled cards was strong on totals/cushions, weaker on exact-winner leans. WINS â€” Cleveland 4-3/10inn (3-1, both +1.5 + under), Lynx 78-76 (top-4 4-0, perfect under sweep + winner), Scotland-Brazil 14 corners (live Over 9 won). MIXED â€” Portland-Chicago (phase unders won, full under lost to a 55-pt Chicago 2H blowout â†’ new full-total 2H-blowout-tail rule), Yankees-Tigers (live Yankees +1.5 won but Detroit winner lean lost; Skubal gave up 3 HRs â†’ returning-ace exact-winner caution). MISSES â€” Braves-Padres (cold Atlanta swept, Braves +1.5 + winner lost; #1 stress test had predicted it â†’ cold-offense +1.5 fade rule), South Africa-South Korea (must-win RSA beat can-draw KOR 1-0; non-loss top pick inverted â†’ must-win vs can-draw nuance). Net theme: totals/under and +1.5-in-low-run reads were reliable; exact-winner and "non-loss safety" picks were the leak. All lessons logged to sports doc.
- 2026-06-25 WNBA elite-home-defense correction: Golden State beat Atlanta 77-66 (Q1 40, 1H 71, full 143), so the top-stacked overs all lost and all three unders won. When a high-pace road offense visits a slow, league-best home defense and is also missing a frontcourt scorer, anchor the total to the home opponent-PPG number, not the visitor's recent scoring spike; this is the defensive mirror of the Aces-Storm all-unders failure. Logged to sports doc as the WNBA elite-home-defense ANCHOR rule.
- 2026-06-27 pending cleanup: Manawatu-Otago 104-97 and Seattle-Washington 227/6 showed that single suppressors (one scorer out, one prior low Oakland game) cannot outweigh direct H2H pace, weak defence and live batting/death-hitting ceiling. Brisbane 126-83 over Sydney and Tampa Bay 13-2 over Kansas City also reinforced that live/home-favourite blowout paths must demote cute underdog-cushion top picks.
- 2026-06-27 WNBA Dallas-Las Vegas settlement: Aces won 99-84, Q1 45, 1H 88, full 183. The 1H over and Q1 over were right, but full Under 178.5 missed because direct H2H full/phase pace plus Dallas missing Alanna Smith created an A'ja Wilson/frontcourt efficiency path. Do not let a recent Aces under streak alone rank full under above the over when direct matchup and injury mismatch point to 180+.
- 2026-06-27 WT20I Scotland-Sri Lanka settlement: Scotland made 151/6 with a 45-run powerplay, so the modest 40.5 powerplay over and 140.5 full-innings over both cleared. At 140.5-style Scotland lines, the under remains live through middle-over stalls, but do not rank it above the over when Carter/Fraser powerplay form, current tournament 146/151/161/162 scoring evidence and a dry Manchester setup support 145-155.
- 2026-06-27 AFL Carlton-West Coast correction: Carlton won 117-64, so the favourite cover and over both won while the pregame underdog cushion/under failed. This reinforces, rather than replaces, the existing AFL large-favourite rule: recent low totals and zero recent 36+ wins are not enough to top-rank the dog plus under if the favourite has a clean early-territory, percentage and weak-opponent blowout path.
- 2026-06-27 AFL live-state correction: Collingwood beat Richmond 106-72 after the card moved from an early tied-under/dog read to a Q4 over/favourite-cover re-rank. The final over landed at 178, but Richmond +38.5 still covered because a 24-point Tigers Q4 reduced the final margin to 34. Lesson: late live AFL re-ranks must separate total arithmetic from spread arithmetic; when the favourite is only 2-3 points beyond the spread with several minutes left, do not treat favourite cover as safe just because the over is now favoured.
- 2026-06-27 WT20I Pakistan-Netherlands settlement: Pakistan made only 126/6 after a 34/1 powerplay and still won by bowling Netherlands out for 89. The top two unders won and the bottom two overs lost, reinforcing that Netherlands' prior concessions to elite batting sides should not be transferred to Pakistan-tier batting at a 159 line. For non-elite WT20I batting, an "excellent/good" Bristol surface still needs a clear 46+ powerplay plus wickets-in-hand path before the full-innings over outranks the under.
- 2026-06-28 WT20I West Indies-Ireland correction: West Indies made only 128/7 after a 40/2 powerplay on a pitch report that included "excellent batting track" language, and Ireland chased 129/4. Do not let a favourable pitch phrase and one short boundary override same-day low powerplays, the batting side's slow-start history, green/dry/overhead caveats, and a credible new-ball/seam route. For 150s WT20I lines, wickets in hand and the first-six score are the main ceiling controls.
- 2026-06-28 pending cleanup: England chased New Zealand's 164 target at 164/1 in 17.2 overs with a 57/1 powerplay. The target-cap Under 164.5 won exactly as structured, but the PP Under 52.5 lost; chase caps do not cap elite first-six aggression on a good surface.
- 2026-06-28 MLB pending cleanup: Cincinnati-Pittsburgh blew past the rain-delay under at 9-7 after four HRs and a three-run Reds ninth; Chicago beat Kansas City 2-1; Tampa Bay beat Arizona 4-2. The durable lessons: re-check post-delay run environment before treating rain/wind-in as an under anchor, protect both +1.5 cushions in low-run one-run MLB games, and keep dome/current-under stacks above opener/bulk over tails when the under path is supported by rolling totals.
- 2026-06-29 cricket pending cleanup: Bangladesh made 117/5 with a 23-run powerplay, Ireland made 154/8 with a 49-run powerplay, and India Women made 170/4 with a 43-run powerplay. The key repeat lesson is phase/full split discipline: early wickets can make a low Bangladesh line stay under, a men's T20 powerplay over can still clear by 0.5 despite innings under, and an elite women's batting side can clear a modest full-innings over without clearing a high-ish first-six line if wickets are preserved for a late surge.
- 2026-06-30 FIBA Asian Qualifiers cleanup: Qatar-Saudi Arabia finished 80-86 with Q1 49, 1H 81 and full 166, validating full/1H overs but exposing the Qatar winner lean and the near-line Q1 under. Iraq-Jordan finished 59-108 with Q1 51, 1H 90 and full 167, a poor under stack despite the correct Jordan winner. For FIBA qualifier totals, do not stack phase/full unders from old H2H/control narratives when the current window already shows Iraq-style defensive overs, Jordan/Saudi qualification urgency, and favourite blowout/turnover paths.
- 2026-06-30 World Cup Brazil-Japan cleanup: Brazil beat Japan 2-1 and corners landed exactly 8 (Brazil 6, Japan 2), so all four listed legs won. Keep broad knockout soccer safety legs above exact side/score conviction, and treat corner overs at 7.5/8.0 as gradeable but fragile when they clear by the minimum.
- 2026-07-01 KBO pending cleanup: SSG and KIA drew 6-6 after 11 innings. The Over 10.5 and SSG +1.5 were correct, but the Tigers ML/top winner lean lost because KBO tie rules and late bullpen volatility kept the exact favourite-win path from closing. For KBO, never rank an exact ML above a broad cushion or live over solely from starter/team-form edge unless the tie/no-decision branch has been explicitly priced.
- 2026-07-05 cricket pending cleanup: India made 190/7 with a 65-run powerplay, so both pregame unders won while both overs lost. The PP Under 66.5 won by only 1.5, so this is correct-but-fragile phase calibration rather than a strong under proof. Reinforce the existing cricket phase/full split: a full-innings under at 195.5 can be clean while the powerplay under near 66.5 is still a near-line leg when elite T20 openers can reach the 63-67 band.
- 2026-07-06 NRLW pending cleanup: Wests Tigers beat Parramatta 28-16 after leading 22-0 at halftime. The Eels cover/under top half failed and the bottom Tigers +7.5 plus over won. Do not anchor NRLW Round 1 too heavily to prior-season H2H when the underdog has a new half/spine and middle recruits while the favourite is missing a major forward; keep underdog cushions adjacent and treat attack-upgrade overs as live.
- 2026-07-06 WT20I final settlement (England-Australia at Lord's): England made 150/4 with a 39/2 powerplay; Australia chased 151 at 153/3 in 17.1 (won by 7 wkts, record 7th title). The full-innings Under 160.5 (#1) and the Australia winner call were both right â€” elite Australian attack + final pressure capped England, as read. The miss was ranking PP Over 45.5 (#2) above PP Under (#3): England slumped to 39/2 vs Garth/Hamilton's new ball. New cricket lesson: the generic "do not bury modest powerplay overs" rule is OVERRIDDEN when an inserted batting side faces an Australia-tier elite pace new-ball attack under final/knockout pressure â€” rank the PP Under at or above the PP Over there, because the batting side's PP outputs against weaker attacks (England's 46/51/57) do not transfer against elite quality.
- Active settlement policy: keep live/pending rows in table format, update the result cell with the freshest reliable state, and settle only from official or reliable full-game/phase sources.
- Active row-construction policy: include a potential game winner/advance lean whenever the fixture has a meaningful side outcome, and settle that winner honestly even when the listed market picks are totals, phases, corners, or props.

Lesson added: at men's T20 PP lines around 48.5, keep the over adjacent unless there is confirmed severe seam/wetness or a weakened top order; a PP over and full-innings under can win together when early boundaries are followed by middle/death wickets. Repeat lesson: in knockout soccer, keep regulation low-total/non-loss/corners separate from advance/winner confidence unless the finishing and chance-quality edge is much cleaner. |

Closest settled soccer lessons: Argentina beat Egypt 3-2, where the high corner over and Under 3.5 failed; Switzerland-Colombia was 0-0 through regulation/extra time with Switzerland advancing on penalties; Spain-Belgium showed that a favourite team-corner floor can coexist with opponent-corner suppression and a failed match-corner over. RULESET LOCK: single-leg FIFA World Cup quarter-final; tied regulation proceeds to two 15-minute extra-time periods and penalties. CONFIRMED ARGENTINA XI: Emiliano Martinez; Molina, Romero, Lisandro Martinez, Tagliafico; Paredes, De Paul, Mac Allister, Enzo Fernandez; Messi, Julian Alvarez. CONFIRMED SWITZERLAND XI: Kobel; Zakaria, Elvedi, Akanji, Ricardo Rodriguez; Freuler, Xhaka, Rieder, Sow; Ndoye, Embolo. AVAILABILITY: Argentina had no confirmed out in the final team-news packet; Facundo Medina was doubtful but starts on the bench. Switzerland's three-goal tournament scorer Johan Manzambi was confirmed out; Aebischer and Jaquez were doubts and are on the bench. FORM/ACTUAL N: only five World Cup matches exist for each current-tournament sample, so L10/L15/L20 tournament windows are MISSING and confidence is capped. Argentina regulation sequence: 3-0 Algeria, 2-0 Austria, 3-1 Jordan, 1-1 Cabo Verde before extra time, 3-2 Egypt (12 GF, 4 GA). Switzerland regulation sequence: 1-1 Qatar, 4-1 Bosnia-Herzegovina, 2-1 Canada, 2-0 Algeria, 0-0 Colombia (9 GF, 3 GA). Broader anchors: Argentina were unbeaten in 11 World Cup matches and had scored in 14 straight; Switzerland had lost only one of 19 internationals, and Argentina's H2H was five wins/two draws with no Swiss win. PRESS SIGNAL: TACTICAL/MOTIVATION, low weight. Yakin publicly called Argentina vulnerable after their last two matches; Scaloni said the XI would be similar and framed Argentina around persistence. These support a competitive Swiss route but do not override the lineup/quality edge. OFFICIALS: Joao Pinheiro referee, Bruno Jesus/Luciano Maia assistants; no quantified card or penalty adjustment used. CONDITIONS: clear around 28C at the 20:00 local start, easing later; warm but no rain mechanism. GOAL MODEL: independent Poisson diagnostic lambdas Argentina 1.65, Switzerland 0.80 were chosen to reproduce Opta's 25,000-simulation regulation baseline (57.7% Argentina/24.3% draw/18.0% Switzerland). Raw outputs: Argentina O0.5 80.8%, Switzerland U2.5 95.3%, Argentina double chance 81.9%, full U4.5 89.8%. Dixon-Coles/dynamic dependence is the preferred conceptual challenger; Opta's external simulation was used as a calibrated sport-native check. CORNER MODEL: raw Poisson rejected because corners are overdispersed and game-state dependent. Negative-binomial gamma-Poisson mixture, mean 10.0 and dispersion k=8, gave U13.5 about 78.8%. FIFA's tournament average was 9.16 corners per game; opened official reports included Argentina-Egypt 7, Argentina-Austria 4, Jordan-Argentina 8, Argentina-Cabo Verde 20 over 120 minutes, Switzerland-Colombia 10 over 120 and Qatar-Switzerland 13. Regulation L5 corner coverage was PARTIAL, so the corner leg remains fourth. SIMULATION: 100,000 runs at seeds 260712/260713/260714; ranges were Switzerland U2.5 95.18-95.21%, Argentina DC 81.84-81.99%, Argentina O0.5 80.77-80.96%, corners U13.5 78.70-78.93%. Goal mean 2.45, median 2, SD 1.56, P10/P25/P75/P90 1/1/3/5; corner mean 10.0, SD 4.74, P10/P25/P75/P90 4/7/13/16. FRAMEWORK SWEEP: combined sports doc, v2/v3 logs, calibration ledger, source registry and Poisson framework checked; independent Poisson retained only as transparent goal baseline, Dixon-Coles/Opta used for dependence/calibration, NB selected for corners. #1 STRESS: Switzerland score three if Argentina's recent high conversion allowed from only nine SOT persists, Embolo/Ndoye exploit transitions, and set pieces stack; Manzambi's absence lowers but does not remove this tail. #4 STRESS: Under 13.5 corners fails if Switzerland score first and Argentina's crossing/blocked-shot chase resembles the 20-corner Cabo Verde match, or if both sides repeatedly attack wide. WINNER RISK: Argentina's regulation win probability is only about 58%; the advance lean rises near 70% after ET/penalties but Switzerland just won a shootout behind Kobel, so this is not a lock. |

ARITHMETIC: Over 160.5 needs 85 second-half points; Under 180.5 loses only if the second half reaches 105. A repeat of the 76-point first half finishes 152, making Under the cleaner threshold. TERRITORY: Brisbane led inside 50s 37-19, marks inside 50 9-5, tackles inside 50 10-0, clearances 21-17 and scoring shots 19-7. Brisbane's 64 came from 9.10, so its conversion was not unsustainably hot; Essendon's one-goal attack is the main drag on the low Over. BLOWOUT CORRECTION: Brisbane's 52-point lead activates the stored AFL live rule that a 50+ halftime lead invalidates a passive pregame under anchor because favourite territory, transition and opponent defensive collapse can create a large late score. That keeps Over 160.5 above a coin-flip fade rather than burying it. UNDER EDGE: even with an 85-95 point second half, the final lands around 161-171 and both selections win; Under 180.5 has a much larger allowance and loses through a 105+ second half, requiring Brisbane to keep attacking deep into the rout and/or Essendon to find a material scoring rebound. OVER FAILURE: Brisbane manage the margin, rotations reduce pressure and Essendon remain near one-to-three goals. UNDER FAILURE: Brisbane chase percentage, sustain the 37-19 inside-50 dominance and add 65-75 while Essendon improve by 30-40. MODEL: live remaining-points scenario distribution centred high-80s/low-90s with a wide blowout tail; raw Poisson rejected because AFL scoring is compound, phase dependent and margin affected. HONESTY: probabilities are conditional estimates, not guarantees; both markets are not opposites. |

FORM, official fallback after StatMuse was stale/incomplete: MIL L5/L10/L15/L20 RF-RA-total 5.4-4.2-9.6 / 4.8-3.7-8.5 / 4.6-3.67-8.27 / 4.35-3.55-7.9; +1.5 hits 4/5, 9/10, 13/15, 17/20; Over 8.5 hits 3/5, 4/10, 6/15, 8/20. BULLPEN/REST: yesterday's split doubleheader required four Milwaukee relievers in Game 1 and two in Game 2; Pittsburgh used two and five respectively. Key recent pitch counts included MIL Ashby 24, Yoho 25, Patrick 18, Uribe 13; PIT Mlodzinski 57, Soto 20, Santana 19, Mattson 24, Montgomery 27. This raises late variance and is the main Over path. CONDITIONS/OFFICIALS: MLB feed 82F, sunny, 5 mph left-to-right; neutral-to-warm, no strong wind-out mechanism. Alfonso Marquez home plate. PRESS SIGNAL: current tactical comment not found; official injury reporting confirms Misiorowski fatigue and Gasser replacement, an AVAILABILITY SIGNAL that weakens Milwaukee's run-prevention/winner ceiling. MODEL: conservative independent-run baseline MIL 4.0, PIT 4.3 (8.3 total) returned about PIT +1.5 74%, MIL +1.5 67%, Under 8.5 55%, Over 45%, PIT winner 55% after an estimated home extras tiebreak; probability bands widened for bullpen fatigue and starter-form disagreement. #1 stress: Pirates +1.5 fails if current Skenes command problems recur and Milwaukee's top four create an early multi-run lead that survives the taxed bullpen. #4 slot: Over is FORCED BOTTOM, not structurally dead; it wins if either starter exits near five innings or yesterday's bullpen traffic repeats. SPORT-LEAK CHECK: MLB bottom slots have historically leaked, so confidence is capped and Over remains a live 45-47% complement. |

## Update Template

| YYYY-MM-DD | Sport | Team A vs Team B @ venue | Market group and settlement convention | 1. Pick (prob %, source-backed mechanism), 2. Pick, 3. Pick, 4. Pick; potential winner/result if relevant | Final score/state plus required phase splits | Listed W-L-P; top/bottom penalties or exemptions; pending/void/ungraded flags | Request/cutoff time; GAME-STATE; RULESET LOCK; opened sources with timestamps/access/status tags; eligibility/classification verdict; settlement/dispute status; conditions/hidden factors; stat matrix and recency/baseline deltas; form delta table verdict + form anchor; press signal class/outcome; officials line; source confidence/disagreement; similar-condition form; role/replacement/bench; style fit; volatility/stat stability; motivation/news/market context; prediction triggers; projection band; probability table; #1 stress test; #4 failure paths/swap test; pass/no-edge note; miss-cause flags; concrete repeat/change lesson. |

Expanded pre-card block to paste into each new row before shortening into the table:

```text
REQUEST/CUTOFF:
GAME-STATE: PREGAME / LIVE / LIKELY COMPLETE - local start, AEST start, request time.
FIXTURE/FORMAT:
RULESET LOCK: competition, governing body, rulebook/playing-conditions edition, competition variant, season phase.
SETTLEMENT CONVENTIONS: OT included / 90 minutes only / push number / DLS-sensitive / abandoned handling.
SETTLEMENT MATRIX: overtime, extra time, penalties/shootout, Super Over, DLS, reserve day, automatic runner, suspended-game continuation, official correction, protest/review correction.
OFFICIAL SOURCE ORDER: rulebook/playing conditions -> competition regulations -> official match centre/gamebook/scorecard -> verified stats/reporting.
SOURCES OPENED: source - timestamp/recency - access/status tag - fact used.
ELIGIBILITY / CLASSIFICATION CHECK: roster/team-sheet deadline, starters/XI/active list, suspensions, age/category/import clearance, concussion/HIA, tiebreak/classification if relevant.
AVAILABILITY VERDICT:
CONDITIONS/HIDDEN FACTORS: venue, weather, officials, travel/rest, motivation, surface/roof, tactical comments.
CRICKET SURFACE EVIDENCE: ACTUAL PITCH REPORT: source/wording or UNKNOWN; CAPTAIN/TOSS COMMENTS: source/wording or none; LIVE SURFACE BEHAVIOUR: score/time trigger or not applicable.
STAT COVERAGE MATRIX: checked / MISSING / not relevant per stat; all four windows L5/L10/L15/L20 REQUIRED on decision-driving stats; missing L5/L10 caps confidence.
FORM DELTA TABLE: stat | L5 | L10 | L15 | L20 | SEASON | HIST(level) | d5 | d10 | dH | verdict.
FORM ANCHOR: window/subsample + reason.
PRESS SIGNAL: none found / not opened / S-class + speaker + role + date + source + quote -> direction hypothesis -> weight applied.
OFFICIALS: names + role + source + timestamp + access tag / NOT YET ASSIGNED / SOURCE UNAVAILABLE.
SOURCE CONFIDENCE / DISAGREEMENT:
SIMILAR-CONDITION FORM:
ROLE / REPLACEMENT / BENCH:
STYLE FIT:
VOLATILITY / STAT STABILITY:
MOTIVATION / NEWS / MARKET CONTEXT:
PREDICTION TRIGGERS:
PROJECTION FIRST: market -> projected band -> line distance -> coinflip flag if near line.
FRAMEWORK SWEEP: sports doc + v2/v3 logs + ledger + source registry + Poisson framework; closest lessons and conflicts.
MODEL TARGET / EXPOSURE:
POISSON BASELINE / DIAGNOSTICS:
CHALLENGER MODEL(S) / SELECTED MODEL + WHY:
TRAINING WINDOW / DECAY / INPUT CUTOFF:
SIMULATIONS / SEED:
MEAN / MEDIAN / SD / P10 / P25 / P75 / P90:
OVER / UNDER / PUSH / VOID / OUTCOME PROBABILITIES:
MODEL DISAGREEMENT / CALIBRATION STATUS:
PROBABILITY TABLE:
#1 STRESS TEST:
#4 SLOT TEST:
PASS/NO-EDGE:
FINAL RANKING:
DISPUTE / OFFICIAL-CORRECTION CHECK:
SETTLEMENT UPDATE:
RETROSPECTIVE AUDIT CAUSE:
```
